# UE5 Python API Reference (this build)

Engine: `5.7.4-51494982+++UE5+Release-5.7`. Generated by `Content/Python/generate_api_reference.py`.

Each section is the live `__doc__` of the class on THIS build (methods with
signatures + brief descriptions). This is a curated subset — for any class use
the `python/inspect_class`, `python/search_symbol`, `python/get_function_signature`
tools, or `dir(unreal.<Class>)` / `help(unreal.<Class>.<method>)` in the console.

See also `ue5_python_best_practices.md` (golden rules + subsystem map) and
`deprecations.md` (deprecated -> modern API).

# Editor subsystems (unreal.get_editor_subsystem / get_engine_subsystem)

## unreal.EditorActorSubsystem

**Best practices:** The UE5 replacement for the deprecated unreal.EditorLevelLibrary actor/outliner functions. Get it via unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.EditorActorSubsystem) — never construct directly. The methods that matter: get_all_level_actors(), get_selected_level_actors()/set_selected_level_actors(list), spawn_actor_from_class(cls, location, rotation)/spawn_actor_from_object(obj, ...), duplicate_actor(actor, to_world, offset), destroy_actor()/destroy_actors(). Locations are in cm and rotations in degrees (FRotator). Gotchas: must run on the Game Thread (all UE Python does); it operates on the current editor world, so call unreal.UnrealEditorSubsystem().get_editor_world() if you need to be explicit. Spawns/deletes are individually undoable but for batches wrap in `with unreal.ScopedEditorTransaction("...")` so the Outliner coalesces redraws and the whole op is one undo. For idempotent scripts, query get_all_level_actors() and skip spawning if a matching actor already exists rather than spawn-then-dedupe. Note (5.5+): editor subsystems can throw 'Editor Subsystems can only be used in Editor Utilities' inside Scriptable Tools blueprints — fine from normal py/exec.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/EditorActorSubsystem_

## unreal.UnrealEditorSubsystem

**Best practices:** Tiny but essential subsystem for global editor app/world state — the UE5 home for the world/viewport-camera functions that moved off EditorLevelLibrary. Only four methods, all of which you actually use: get_editor_world() (the UWorld you edit; pass this to other subsystems that take a UWorld), get_game_world() (only non-None during PIE), and get_level_viewport_camera_info(out location, out rotation)/set_level_viewport_camera_info(location, rotation). Camera location is cm, rotation degrees. Gotcha: get_level_viewport_camera_info returns a (bool, FVector, FRotator) tuple in Python (the bool is success) — unpack accordingly. get_editor_world() is the canonical 'current world' accessor; prefer it over the deprecated unreal.EditorLevelLibrary.get_editor_world().  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/UnrealEditorSubsystem_

```text
UUnrealEditorSubsystem
Subsystem for exposing editor functionality to scripts

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: UnrealEd
- **File**: UnrealEditorSubsystem.h
```

## unreal.LevelEditorSubsystem

**Best practices:** Owns level-file and viewport/PIE operations (the rest of what EditorLevelLibrary did). Key methods: new_level(asset_path, is_partitioned_world=False)/new_level_from_template(), load_level(asset_path), save_current_level(), save_all_dirty_levels(), get_current_level()/set_current_level_by_name(), and the PIE controls editor_request_begin_play()/editor_request_end_play()/is_in_play_in_editor(). Also build_light_maps(quality, with_reflection_captures) and pilot/eject camera helpers. Gotchas: load_level / new_level swap the editor's current map — destructive, prompts/loses unsaved changes, and invalidates actor references you captured from the previous world, so re-query actors after a level change. Saves write to disk (slow, can trigger recompiles for level-embedded assets); guard with the dirty checks (save_all_dirty_levels already no-ops clean levels). For World Partition maps set is_partitioned_world=True on new_level. Get via unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.LevelEditorSubsystem).  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/LevelEditorSubsystem_

## unreal.EditorAssetSubsystem

**Best practices:** The UE5 replacement for the deprecated unreal.EditorAssetLibrary; same method names, accessed via unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.EditorAssetSubsystem). Bread-and-butter: load_asset(path), does_asset_exist(path)/do_assets_exist(list) (use these for idempotency before any rebuild), list_assets(dir, recursive, include_folder), duplicate_asset/rename_asset/delete_asset (path-string variants) and their *_loaded_asset counterparts (object variants), save_loaded_asset(asset, only_if_is_dirty=True)/save_asset(path, only_if_is_dirty=True), set_dirty_flag(obj, state), find_asset_data(path), and metadata: get_metadata_tag/set_metadata_tag/get_tag_values. Critical gotchas: keep only_if_is_dirty=True (the default) — passing False forces a disk write and can re-trigger a shader/asset recompile even when nothing changed. delete_asset cascades a recompile of every referencer, so fix up find_package_referencers_for_asset first; never delete+recreate just to re-run a script. Path-string ops (rename/duplicate/delete) don't need the asset loaded and are cheaper than loaded-object variants for bulk work. save_* persists to .uasset — without it your in-memory edits are lost on editor restart.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/EditorAssetSubsystem_

## unreal.EditorUtilitySubsystem

**Best practices:** Runs Editor Utility Blueprints/Widgets and registers their tabs — use it to launch existing Blutility tools from script, not for general asset/actor work. Practical methods: try_run(asset)/try_run_class(cls) to execute an Editor Utility Blueprint, spawn_and_register_tab(widget_blueprint) to open an Editor Utility Widget as a docked tab, register_and_execute_task(task) for long-running UEditorUtilityTask jobs, and the tab lifecycle helpers (does_tab_exist/close_tab_by_id/unregister_tab_by_id). Gotcha: this is the entry point for 'run my EUW/EUB from Python', not a substitute for EditorActorSubsystem/EditorAssetSubsystem — most scripting needs those instead. Tabs you spawn persist in the editor UI until explicitly closed. Get via unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.EditorUtilitySubsystem).  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/EditorUtilitySubsystem_

## unreal.StaticMeshEditorSubsystem

**Best practices:** Scripts the Static Mesh editor: LODs, collision, Nanite, sections, UV channels, material/mesh swaps. Get via unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.StaticMeshEditorSubsystem). High-value methods: set_lods_with_notification(mesh, FStaticMeshReductionOptions, apply_changes), set_lod_group(mesh, name, rebuild_immediately=True), set_nanite_settings(mesh, FMeshNaniteSettings, apply_changes=True), add_simple_collisions_with_notification(mesh, shape_type, apply_changes)/set_convex_decomposition_collisions_with_notification(...), import_lod(mesh, lod_index, source_filename), get_lod_count/get_number_verts/get_number_materials. Critical gotcha: the apply_changes (a.k.a. rebuild_immediately) flag triggers a full mesh build + shader/derived-data rebuild — expensive and blocks the Game Thread. When changing several settings on one mesh, pass apply_changes=False on all but the last call so you rebuild once, not N times. After editing you still must EditorAssetSubsystem.save_loaded_asset() to persist. The non-notification variants (add_simple_collisions, remove_collisions, etc.) always apply immediately — prefer the *_with_notification forms for batched edits. Wrap visible edits in ScopedEditorTransaction for undo. UE5.7 deprecation: reimport_all_custom_lo_ds was renamed reimport_all_custom_lods.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/StaticMeshEditorSubsystem_

```text
**C++ Source:**
```

## unreal.SkeletalMeshEditorSubsystem

**Best practices:** Skeletal-mesh counterpart to StaticMeshEditorSubsystem: LODs, sections, sockets, physics assets, morph targets. Get via unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.SkeletalMeshEditorSubsystem). Useful methods: get_lod_count/regenerate_lod(mesh, new_lod_count, ...)/import_lod/remove_lods, get_lod_build_settings/set_lod_build_settings, rename_socket(mesh, old, new), create_physics_asset(mesh, set_to_mesh=True, lod_index=0)/assign_physics_asset(mesh, physics_asset)/is_physics_asset_compatible, set_skeletal_mesh_overlay_material/set_material_slot_overlay_material, and per-section getters/setters (cast shadow, recompute tangent, visible in ray tracing) that return values via out-params (Python returns tuples — unpack the bool success plus the value). Gotcha: many getters take an out-param in C++ so in Python you read the returned tuple, e.g. ok, cast = sub.get_section_cast_shadow(mesh, lod, section). LOD regen/import rebuilds the mesh (heavy, Game Thread). Persist edits with EditorAssetSubsystem.save_loaded_asset(); wrap in ScopedEditorTransaction for undo.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/SkeletalMeshEditorSubsystem_

## unreal.SubobjectDataSubsystem

**Best practices:** The ONLY supported way to add/remove/reparent components on a Blueprint asset's component tree from script (you cannot just call add_component on a UBlueprint). It is an ENGINE subsystem: get it with unreal.get_engine_subsystem(unreal.SubobjectDataSubsystem), NOT get_editor_subsystem. Core flow: root_handle = sub.k2_gather_subobject_data_for_blueprint(blueprint)[0]; then handle, fail = sub.add_new_subobject(unreal.AddNewSubobjectParams(parent_handle=root_handle, new_class=unreal.StaticMeshComponent, blueprint_context=blueprint)); check fail.is_empty(); sub.rename_subobject(handle, unreal.Text(name)); sub.attach_subobject(root_handle, handle). Key concept: methods return/consume SubobjectDataHandle (an opaque id, NOT the component) — to get the actual component object use unreal.SubobjectDataBlueprintFunctionLibrary.get_object(get_data(handle)) and set_editor_property on it. For live actor instances (not BP assets) use k2_gather_subobject_data_for_instance(actor) and pass blueprint_context=None. Gotchas: after editing the Blueprint you must compile + EditorAssetSubsystem.save_loaded_asset() to persist; add_new_subobject does not save. add_new_subobject returns a 2-tuple (handle, fail_reason FText) — always test fail_reason.is_empty().  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/SubobjectDataSubsystem_

```text
The Subobject Data Subsystem will produce the reflected subobject data
based on a given root object. A root object can be anything, an actor
instance clicked on via the level editor, a UBlueprint* by opening an asset,
or something piped in from python or other scripting languages.

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: SubobjectDataInterface
- **File**: SubobjectDataSubsystem.h
```

## unreal.LayersSubsystem

**Best practices:** Manages editor visibility Layers (the Layers panel) and actor-to-layer membership. Get via unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.LayersSubsystem). Most-used: create_layer(name), add_actor_to_layer(actor, name)/add_actors_to_layers(actors, names), remove_actor_from_layer(...), get_actors_from_layer(name)/get_actors_from_layers(names), set_layer_visibility(name, is_visible)/make_all_layers_visible(), select_actors_in_layer(name, select, notify, ...), delete_layer/rename_layer. Gotchas: Layers are a pure editor organizational/visibility concept — they have no runtime effect and are largely superseded by the World Partition Data Layers system in partitioned maps (use Data Layers there, not this). create_layer is idempotent-friendly: check get_layer(name)/is_layer(name) first. Operates on the current editor world. Note many methods take a trailing update_stats/notify/redraw bool — leave defaults unless batching, where suppressing redraws speeds bulk edits.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/LayersSubsystem_

## unreal.AssetEditorSubsystem

**Best practices:** Opens and closes the per-asset editor windows (Material editor, BP editor, etc.) — UI control only, not asset data editing. Get via unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.AssetEditorSubsystem). The methods you need: open_editor_for_assets(list_of_loaded_objects) to pop open editor tabs, close_all_editors_for_asset(obj), close_all_asset_editors(), and find_editor_for_asset/get_all_edited_assets for introspection. Gotcha: takes loaded UObjects (EditorAssetSubsystem.load_asset(path) first), not path strings. Don't confuse with EditorAssetSubsystem (data ops) — this one just manages windows. Opening editors spins up Slate UI on the Game Thread; for headless/batch asset processing you almost never want this, edit the asset object directly and save instead. Useful to force an asset's editor closed before a destructive rename/delete so stale tabs don't linger.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/AssetEditorSubsystem_


## unreal.EditorActorFolders

**Best practices:** WARNING: this class is NOT exposed to the Python API in UE5.7 — unreal.EditorActorFolders does not exist (the underlying FActorFolders is a plain C++ struct accessed via FActorFolders::Get(), not a UObject/subsystem, and its old FName-based CreateFolder/DeleteFolder/RenameFolderInWorld methods were deprecated in 5.0 in favor of FFolder). Do not try to get_editor_subsystem(unreal.EditorActorFolders) — it will raise. To organize actors into Outliner folders from Python, set the folder path directly on each actor: actor.set_folder_path(unreal.Name('Parent/Child')) (or set_folder_path_recursively); read it back with actor.get_folder_path(). Creating a folder is implicit — assigning a path that doesn't exist yet creates it. For World Partition maps, folders are persisted as UActorFolder objects automatically when you set actor folder paths.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/Actor_

_(not available on this build)_

# Asset / content helper libraries (classmethods)

## unreal.AssetToolsHelpers

**Best practices:** Thin accessor only: call `unreal.AssetToolsHelpers.get_asset_tools()` once to get the singleton `AssetTools` object — that returned object is where the real work lives (`create_asset(name, package_path, asset_class, factory)`, `create_unique_asset_name(base_package_name, suffix)`, `duplicate_asset(name, path, source_obj)`, `rename_assets`/`rename_assets_with_dialog`, `import_assets`/`import_asset_tasks`). For idempotent scripts, ALWAYS guard `create_asset` with `unreal.EditorAssetLibrary.does_asset_exist(...)` and `load_asset` the existing one instead — `create_asset` over an existing path can clobber or error. Use `create_unique_asset_name` to dodge collisions in batch creation. In headless/commandlet automation NEVER use the `*_with_dialog` variants (they block); pick factories that import unattended (e.g. avoid the interactive `FbxFactory` dialog). Must run on the game thread; new assets are dirty-in-memory — persist with `EditorAssetLibrary.save_asset` or `save_loaded_asset` afterward.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/AssetTools_

```text
- **Module**: AssetTools
```

## unreal.AssetRegistryHelpers

**Best practices:** The query/inspection layer for UNLOADED assets — pairs with `unreal.AssetRegistryHelpers.get_asset_registry()` (returns the `AssetRegistry` where `get_assets_by_path`/`get_assets_by_class`/`get_all_assets`/`get_referencers`/`run_assets_discovery_gathering` live). The helper itself operates on `FAssetData`: `get_asset(asset_data)` (loads on demand), `get_class`, `to_soft_object_path`, `get_tag_value`, `is_valid`, `is_redirector`, `is_u_asset`, plus `get_blueprint_assets`/`get_derived_class_asset_data`/`find_asset_native_class` for BP-class hierarchy queries without loading. Bridge from a path via `EditorAssetLibrary.find_asset_data(path)`. KEY WIN: reading tag values and classes here is cheap because it does NOT load the asset (avoid the shader/asset-load cost of `load_asset`). Gotcha: on a cold editor the registry may still be scanning — results can be incomplete until discovery finishes; tag/dependency data can have false positives until all assets are loaded+resaved. UE5.7 deprecation: the bool-argument `get_assets_with_outer_for_paths(...)` overload is deprecated in favor of the enum-flags overload.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/AssetRegistryHelpers_

## unreal.EditorAssetLibrary

**Best practices:** The everyday content-browser workhorse for load/save/exist/list/duplicate/rename/delete on assets BY PATH (it auto-loads as needed). The four that matter: `does_asset_exist(path)` (your idempotency guard — check before any expensive rebuild), `load_asset(path)`, `save_asset(path, only_if_is_dirty=True)` / `save_loaded_asset(obj, only_if_is_dirty=True)`, and `list_assets(dir, recursive, include_folder)`. Also `duplicate_asset`, `rename_asset`, `delete_asset`, `find_asset_data` (bridge to AssetRegistryHelpers), and metadata get/set tag helpers. Critical gotchas: `only_if_is_dirty` defaults True — leave it (passing False forces a disk write + recompile every run); `save_asset` already triggers any needed recompile, so do not also call `recompile_material`/`update_material_instance`. ALL operations are slow, must run on the game thread, do NOT work in PIE, and do NOT work on level assets (use EditorLoadingAndSavingUtils/LevelEditorSubsystem for maps). `delete_asset` cascades recompiles onto every referencer — fix up referencers first and never delete just to re-create (breaks idempotency). UE5.7 note: Epic increasingly steers editor scripting toward `unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.EditorAssetSubsystem)`, which mirrors this API 1:1 and is the forward-looking choice; this BlueprintFunctionLibrary remains supported.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/EditorAssetLibrary_

```text
Utility class to do most of the common functionalities with the ContentBrowser.
The AssetRegistryHelpers class has more complex utilities. Use FindAssetData to get a FAssetData from an Asset Path.
The Asset Path can be represented by
            ie. (Reference/Text Path)       StaticMesh'/Game/MyFolder/MyAsset.MyAsset'
            ie. (Full Name)                         StaticMesh /Game/MyFolder/MyAsset.MyAsset
            ie. (Path Name)                         /Game/MyFolder/MyAsset.MyAsset
            ie. (Package Name)                      /Game/MyFolder/MyAsset
The Directory Path can be represented by
            ie. /Game/MyNewFolder/
            ie. /Game/MyNewFolder
All operations can be slow. The editor should not be in play in editor mode. It will not work on assets of the type level.

**C++ Source:**

- **Plugin**: EditorScriptingUtilities
- **Module**: EditorScriptingUtilities
- **File**: EditorAssetLibrary.h
```

## unreal.MaterialEditingLibrary

**Best practices:** The only sanctioned way to build/edit Material graphs and tweak MaterialInstanceConstants from Python. Core flow: `create_material_expression(material, unreal.MaterialExpressionX, x, y)` to add nodes, `connect_material_expressions(from, from_out, to, to_in)` (empty-string out/in names = first pin) and `connect_material_property(from, out_name, unreal.MaterialProperty.MP_BASE_COLOR)` to wire to outputs, then exactly ONE `recompile_material(material)` at the end. For MICs use the `set_material_instance_*_parameter_value` setters; for the parent use `set_material_instance_parent` (guard it — reparenting to the same asset still forces a full MI shader rebuild; compare `mi.get_editor_property('parent')` first). Match `sampler_type` on TextureSample nodes to the texture's compression (Metal/SM6 rejects mismatches at compile). Heavy gotchas: every graph mutation can kick a shader compile — batch all node edits then recompile once; the per-MIC scalar setter `set_material_instance_static_switch_parameter_value` has a `b_update_material_instance` flag so you can defer the rebuild and call `update_material_instance` once. Material edits must dirty packages on the game thread (`create_material_expression_ex` exposes `b_allow_marking_package_dirty`). Some hidden material properties (e.g. WorldPositionOffset) aren't in the `MaterialProperty` enum — needs a workaround lib. Saving the material recompiles, so don't double-compile.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/MaterialEditingLibrary_

```text
Blueprint library for creating/editing Materials

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: MaterialEditor
- **File**: MaterialEditingLibrary.h
```

## unreal.BlueprintEditorLibrary

**Best practices:** Edits Blueprint ASSETS (the editor-only `UBlueprint`, not the runtime CDO/instance) — graphs, functions, variables, reparenting. Most-used: `compile_blueprint(bp)` (run after any structural change or the generated class is stale), `create_blueprint_asset_with_parent(asset_path, parent_class)`, `add_member_variable(bp, name, pin_type)` / `add_member_variable_with_value`, and `generated_class(bp)` to get the UClass for spawning/CDO work. Helpers: `get_blueprint_asset(obj)`/`get_blueprint_for_class(cls)` to go between asset and class, `find_event_graph`/`find_graph`/`add_function_graph`/`remove_unused_nodes`/`remove_unused_variables`, `reparent_blueprint`, and `set_blueprint_variable_expose_on_spawn`/`_instance_editable`/`_expose_to_cinematics`. Build pin types with `get_basic_type_by_name`/`get_struct_type`/`get_object_reference_type`/`get_array_type` etc. Gotchas: to read/write a variable's DEFAULT value you go through `generated_class().get_default_object()`, NOT this library; `remove_function_graph` does NOT fix up call sites. Must run on game thread, mark dirty + `EditorAssetLibrary.save_asset` to persist, and always `compile_blueprint` before relying on the new class. UE5.7 adds asset-save-version utilities here (`compare_asset_save_version_to`, `get_saved_by_engine_version`, `get_current_engine_version`) for upgrade tooling.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/BlueprintEditorLibrary_

## unreal.DataTableFunctionLibrary

**Best practices:** Read and (editor-only) rewrite DataTables/CurveTables. Read side (works at runtime too): `get_data_table_row_names`, `get_data_table_column_names`, `does_data_table_row_exist`, `get_data_table_row_struct`, `get_data_table_column_as_string`. Editor-only bulk I/O: `fill_data_table_from_csv_string`/`_csv_file`/`_json_string`/`_json_file` (these EMPTY then refill the whole table — pass the optional `import_row_struct` to force a fully automated import with no dialogs/questions, essential for headless runs) and the matching `export_data_table_to_csv/json_string/file`. Row-level editor ops: `add_data_table_row`/`remove_data_table_row`. Gotchas: column names returned are RAW property names, not the friendly CSV/JSON export names — convert via `get_data_table_column_name_from_export_name`. `add_data_table_row` and `get_data_table_row_from_name` are CustomThunk wildcard nodes — from Python you must pass a struct instance whose type matches the table's row struct or it silently fails. Mutations only take in `WITH_EDITOR`, must run on the game thread, and you must mark the package dirty + `EditorAssetLibrary.save_asset` to persist. `fill_*` returning False means check the Output Log for the import error.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/DataTableFunctionLibrary_

## unreal.EditorUtilityLibrary

**Best practices:** Glue for selection-driven and Editor Utility Widget (Blutility) tools — answers 'what is the user currently pointing at?'. Most-used: `get_selected_assets()` / `get_selected_assets_of_class(cls)` / `get_selected_asset_data()` (content browser selection), `get_selection_set()` (selected ACTORS in the level) and `get_selection_bounds`, plus `get_selected_folder_paths`/`get_current_content_browser_path` for context-aware scripts. Also `sync_browser_to_folders`, a quick `rename_asset(asset, new_name)` (in-place rename only, cannot move folders — use AssetTools/EditorAssetLibrary to relocate), and widget-blueprint authoring helpers (`add_source_widget`, `find_source_widget_by_name`, `convert_to_editor_utility_widget`). The async action classes in the same header (`AsyncEditorDelay`, `AsyncWaitForGameWorld`) let Blutility graphs wait frames/seconds. Gotchas: everything is `WITH_EDITOR`-only and game-thread; selection getters return a live snapshot so capture once at the top of a script rather than re-querying mid-loop; pair with `unreal.EditorUtilitySubsystem` to spawn/run the widgets themselves.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/EditorUtilityLibrary_

```text
Expose editor utility functions to Blutilities

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: Blutility
- **File**: EditorUtilityLibrary.h
```

## unreal.EditorLoadingAndSavingUtils

**Best practices:** Map-level and bulk-package load/save — the piece EditorAssetLibrary deliberately can't do (it refuses level assets). The two that matter for pipelines: `save_dirty_packages(save_map_packages, save_content_packages)` (call once at the END of a batch with both True to flush everything modified) and `load_map(filename)` (force-load, no prompts — the automation-safe variant). Also `save_packages(packages, only_dirty)` for an explicit list, `save_map(world, asset_path)` / `save_current_level`, and `import_scene`/`export_scene`. Gotchas: the `*_with_dialog` variants (`load_map_with_dialog`, `new_map_from_dialog`) BLOCK on UI and will hang or fail in commandlet/headless runs — never use them unattended. Must run on the game thread. For new editor work Epic favors `unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.LevelEditorSubsystem).load_level(path)` for loading; this class's `save_dirty_packages(True, True)` is still the canonical end-of-script save. Load the map BEFORE you mutate/save it in render/build pipelines. (Niche helpers like file-dialog pickers exist but rarely belong in scripts.)  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/EditorLoadingAndSavingUtils_

```text
This class is a wrapper for editor loading and saving functionality
It is meant to contain only functions that can be executed in script (but are also allowed in C++).
It is separated from FEditorFileUtils to ensure new easier to use methods can be created without breaking FEditorFileUtils backwards compatibility
However this should be used in place of FEditorFileUtils wherever possible as the goal is to deprecate FEditorFileUtils eventually

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: UnrealEd
- **File**: FileHelpers.h
```

# Common gameplay / math libraries

## unreal.SystemLibrary

**Best practices:** Maps to C++ UKismetSystemLibrary (Kismet/KismetSystemLibrary.h) — the generic 'utility' grab-bag: validity checks, console commands, traces, debug draw, timers. In editor Python the few methods that earn their keep are `is_valid(object)` (guard before touching possibly-GC'd UObjects), `get_object_name(object)`, and `execute_console_command(world_context_object, command, specific_player=None)` for things with no Python binding (e.g. `r.*` cvars, `obj gc`). Almost every method takes a `world_context_object` first arg: in editor scripts pass `unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.UnrealEditorSubsystem).get_editor_world()` (or any actor) — passing None silently no-ops or errors. GOTCHAS: the trace family (`line_trace_single`, `sphere_trace_single`, etc.) needs a live PIE/game world to hit anything — they return False against a cold editor world with no physics scene, so don't use them for editor geometry queries (use line traces via the world or EditorActorSubsystem instead). Anything latent — `delay`, `retriggerable_delay`, `k2_set_timer*` — is built on the game tick and will NOT fire from a one-shot editor `exec` script; never `delay()` in editor tooling, it just discards the latent action. `print_string` writes to the PIE viewport+log; for editor scripts prefer `unreal.log/log_warning/log_error` so output lands in the LogPython category. Runs on the game thread like all unreal Python.

```text
Kismet System Library

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: Engine
- **File**: KismetSystemLibrary.h
```

## unreal.GameplayStatics

**Best practices:** Maps to C++ UGameplayStatics (Kismet/GameplayStatics.h) — the runtime/gameplay façade: spawn actors, fetch player/pawn/controller/game-mode, play sounds, apply damage, open levels, world time. Most useful in Python: `spawn_actor_from_class(world_context_object, actor_class, location, rotation, ...)`, `get_all_actors_of_class(world_context, actor_class)` / `get_actor_of_class(...)`, `get_player_controller(world_context, index)`, `get_time_seconds(world_context)`. EVERY call needs a real world as `world_context_object`; in editor get it via `unreal.get_editor_subsystem(unreal.UnrealEditorSubsystem).get_editor_world()`. CRITICAL EDITOR GOTCHA: prefer `unreal.EditorActorSubsystem.spawn_actor_from_class(actor_class, location, rotation)` over GameplayStatics for editor tooling — the EditorActorSubsystem path registers the actor with the editor (selection, transactions/undo, marks the level dirty so it persists on save), whereas GameplayStatics spawns a runtime-style actor that the editor doesn't properly track and that won't survive a save cleanly. Pass the class object directly (`unreal.StaticMeshActor` or a loaded BP's `generated_class()`), never `unreal.Class(name=...)`. `get_all_actors_of_class` is O(all actors) — cache the result, don't call it in a loop. Player/pawn/game-mode getters return None outside PIE. `get_all_actors_of_class` is fine read-only; spawn/destroy mutate the level so wrap batches in `unreal.ScopedEditorTransaction` and save the level afterward. Units: cm for location, degrees for FRotator. `begin_spawning_actor_from_blueprint` is deprecated — use the deferred `begin_deferred_actor_spawn_from_class` + `finish_spawning_actor` pair if you need to set properties before BeginPlay.

```text
Static class with useful gameplay utility functions that can be called from both Blueprint and C++

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: Engine
- **File**: GameplayStatics.h
```

## unreal.MathLibrary

**Best practices:** Maps to C++ UKismetMathLibrary (Kismet/KismetMathLibrary.h) — pure, stateless math: vector/rotator/transform/quat ops, interpolation, random, type conversions, easing. No world context, no game-thread mutation, no save concerns — safe to call freely. In Python you rarely need it: native `unreal.Vector`/`unreal.Rotator`/`unreal.Transform`/`unreal.Quat` already overload `+ - * /`, `.length()`, `.normal()`, `.rotation()`, etc., and Python's own `math`/`random` cover scalars, so reach for MathLibrary only for the gameplay-flavored helpers that have no native equivalent: `find_look_at_rotation(start, target)`, `make_rot_from_x/xz(...)`, the `*_interp_to(current, target, delta_time, speed)` family (frame-rate-correct smoothing — needs a real delta-time, meaningless in a one-shot editor script), `random_unit_vector_in_cone_in_degrees(...)`, and `ease(...)`. GOTCHAS: all angles are DEGREES (`make_rotator`, `find_look_at_rotation`), distances cm; `random_*` here draws from the shared engine RNG (non-deterministic across runs) — for reproducible/idempotent generation seed `random.Random(seed)` in Python or use `unreal.MathLibrary.random_*_from_stream` with an explicit `RandomStream`. The many `conv_*` methods are autogenerated Blueprint cast nodes — in Python just construct the target type directly instead.

```text
Kismet Math Library

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: Engine
- **File**: KismetMathLibrary.h
```

## unreal.KismetRenderingLibrary

**Best practices:** Maps to C++ UKismetRenderingLibrary (Kismet/KismetRenderingLibrary.h) — render-target authoring/capture: create/clear/resize RTs, bake a material into an RT, draw via Canvas, read back pixels, export to disk, and (editor-only) convert an RT into a persistent UTexture2D. Workhorses for tooling: `create_render_target2d(world_context, w, h, format=RTF_RGBA16f, clear_color, auto_gen_mips, support_uavs)`, `draw_material_to_render_target(world_context, render_target, material)` (one-shot bake — internally a flush+draw; this is shader/GPU work, the expensive part), and the EDITOR-ONLY bake-out `render_target_create_static_texture2d_editor_only(render_target, name, compression_settings=TC_Default, mip_settings)`. For many draws into one RT use the `begin_draw_canvas_to_render_target` / `end_draw_canvas_to_render_target` pair (each `draw_material_to_render_target` does its own setup/flush — don't loop it). GOTCHAS: all calls need a valid `world_context_object` (editor: `UnrealEditorSubsystem.get_editor_world()`). An RT created via `create_render_target2d` is TRANSIENT — it vanishes on editor restart; to persist visuals you must convert to a UTexture2D (`*_editor_only`) or `export_render_target(...)` to a file, then save the asset. Match `format` to need: `RTF_RGBA8` for color, a float format (RTF_RGBA16f/RTF_R16f) when you read back HDR/linear data via `read_render_target_raw_pixel`. Pixel readback (`read_render_target*`) stalls the game thread on a GPU fence — slow, avoid per-pixel loops; use the bulk `read_render_target_raw(out_samples)`. The `*_editor_only` and `convert_render_target_to_texture2d_editor_only` functions exist only in editor builds. Game thread; this triggers real GPU/shader work so wrap long sequences in ScopedSlowTask and flush via the project's shader-progress helper when timing matters.

_(not available on this build)_

## unreal.KismetMaterialLibrary

**Best practices:** Maps to C++ UKismetMaterialLibrary (Kismet/KismetMaterialLibrary.h) — two distinct jobs: (1) create runtime-tweakable Dynamic Material Instances, and (2) read/write Material Parameter Collection (MPC) globals. Key methods: `create_dynamic_material_instance(world_context, parent, optional_name=NAME_None, creation_flags=EMIDCreationFlags.NONE)` returns a UMaterialInstanceDynamic you then drive with `mid.set_scalar_parameter_value/set_vector_parameter_value/set_texture_parameter_value`; and the MPC pair `set_scalar_parameter_value(world_context, collection, param_name, value)` / `set_vector_parameter_value(...)` plus the `get_*` readers. GOTCHAS: a DMI is a TRANSIENT runtime object — do NOT use it for editor authoring you want to persist. For permanent material edits create/save a real UMaterialInstanceConstant (via AssetTools / `set_material_instance_parent` + `MaterialEditingLibrary.set_material_instance_*_parameter_value`) and save the asset; a DMI is only for live, in-PIE/game tweaks and is thrown away. MPC `set_*_parameter_value` writes a GLOBAL shared by every material sampling that collection — changes are not per-instance and (in editor) won't persist unless you edit and save the MPC asset itself. All five take a `world_context_object` (editor: `UnrealEditorSubsystem.get_editor_world()`). MPC param names must exactly match the collection's defined Scalar/Vector entries or the call silently does nothing. Game thread; setting parameters is cheap (no recompile) — unlike editing the parent material graph, which forces shader recompiles.

_(not available on this build)_

# Actors / components / core types

## unreal.Actor

**Best practices:** Base class for everything placeable in a level; in editor scripting you usually get these from `get_editor_subsystem(unreal.EditorActorSubsystem)` (NOT the deprecated `EditorLevelLibrary`/`EditorActorUtilities` legacy functions). The methods that matter: `get_actor_location/set_actor_location`, `set_actor_transform`, `get_component_by_class`, and reading/writing any UPROPERTY via `get_editor_property`/`set_editor_property` (prefer these over raw attribute assignment so PostEditChange fires and the actor is marked dirty). Units are cm and degrees. GOTCHA: every mutation runs on the game thread, so wrap edits in `with unreal.ScopedEditorTransaction('label'):` so they land on the undo stack and the editor coalesces redraws; call the actor's `modify()` before changing it if you want robust transaction tracking. Spawn via `EditorActorSubsystem.spawn_actor_from_object/spawn_actor_from_class`. To persist level changes you must save the level/map, not `save_asset`.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/scripting-the-unreal-editor-using-python_

```text
Actor is the base class for an Object that can be placed or spawned in a level.
Actors may contain a collection of ActorComponents, which can be used to control how actors move, how they are rendered, etc.
The other main function of an Actor is the replication of properties and function calls across the network during play.


Actor initialization has multiple steps, here's the order of important virtual functions that get called:
- UObject::PostLoad: For actors statically placed in a level, the normal UObject PostLoad gets called both in the editor and during gameplay.
                     This is not called for newly spawned actors.
- UActorComponent::OnComponentCreated: When an actor is spawned in the editor or during gameplay, this gets called for any native components.
                                       For blueprint-created components, this gets called during construction for that component.
                                       This is not called for components loaded from a level.
- AActor::PreRegisterAllComponents: For statically placed actors and spawned actors that have native root components, this gets called now.
                                    For blueprint actors without a native root component, these registration functions get called later during construction.
- UActorComponent::RegisterComponent: All components are registered in editor and at runtime, this creates their physical/visual representation.
                                      These calls may be distributed over multiple frames, but are always after PreRegisterAllComponents.
                                      This may also get called later on after an UnregisterComponent call removes it from the world.
- AActor::PostRegisterAllComponents: Called for all actors both in the editor and in gameplay, this is the last function that is called in all cases.
- AActor::PostActorCreated: When an actor is created in the editor or during gameplay, this gets called right before construction.
                            This is not called for components loaded from a level.
- AActor::UserConstructionScript: Called for blueprints that implement a construction script.
- AActor::OnConstruction: Called at the end of ExecuteConstruction, which calls the blueprint construction script.
                          This is called after all blueprint-created components are fully created and registered.
                          This is only called during gameplay for spawned actors, and may get rerun in the editor when changing blueprints.
- AActor::PreInitializeComponents: Called before InitializeComponent is called on the actor's components.
                                   This is only called during gameplay and in certain editor preview windows.
- UActorComponent::Activate: This will be called only if the component has bAutoActivate set.
                             It will also got called later on if a component is manually activated.
- UActorComponent::InitializeComponent: This will be called only if the component has bWantsInitializeComponentSet.
                                        This only happens once per gameplay session.
- AActor::PostInitializeComponents: Called after the actor's components have been initialized, only during gameplay and some editor previews.
- AActor::BeginPlay: Called when the level starts ticking, only during actual gameplay.
                     This normally happens right after PostInitializeComponents but can be delayed for networked or child actors.
see: https://docs.unrealengine.com/Programming/UnrealArchitecture/Actors
see: https://docs.unrealengine.com/Programming/UnrealArchitecture/Actors/ActorLifecycle
see: UActorComponent

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: Engine
- **File**: Actor.h

**Editor Properties:** (see get_editor_property/set_editor_property)

- ``actor_guid`` (Guid):  [Read-Write] The GUID for this actor; this guid will be the same for actors from instanced streaming levels.
  see: ActorInstanceGuid, FActorInstanceGuidMapper
  note: Don't use VisibleAnywhere here to avoid getting the CPF_Edit flag and get this property reset when resetting to defaults. See FActorDetails::AddActorCategory and EditorUtilities::CopySingleProperty for details.
- ``actor_instance_guid`` (Guid):  [Read-Write] The instance GUID for this actor; this guid will be unique for actors from instanced streaming levels.
  see: ActorGuid
  note: This is not guaranteed to be valid during PostLoad, but safe to access from RegisterAllComponents.
- ``allow_tick_before_begin_play`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether we allow this Actor to tick before it receives the BeginPlay event.
  Normally we don't tick actors until after BeginPlay; this setting allows this behavior to be overridden.
  This Actor must be able to tick for this setting to be relevant.
- ``always_relevant`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Always relevant for network (overrides bOnlyRelevantToOwner).
- ``async_physics_tick_enabled`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether to use use the async physics tick with this actor.
- ``auto_destroy_when_finished`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true then destroy self when "finished", meaning all relevant components report that they are done and no timelines or timers are in flight.
- ``auto_receive_input`` (AutoReceiveInput):  [Read-Write] Automatically registers this actor to receive input from a player.
- ``block_input`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, all input on the stack below this actor will not be considered
- ``call_pre_replication`` (bool):  [Read-Write]
- ``call_pre_replication_for_replay`` (bool):  [Read-Write]
- ``can_be_damaged`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this actor can take damage. Must be true for damage events (e.g. ReceiveDamage()) to be called.
  see: https://www.unrealengine.com/blog/damage-in-ue4
  see: TakeDamage(), ReceiveDamage()
- ``can_be_in_cluster`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor can be put inside of a GC Cluster to improve Garbage Collection performance
- ``content_bundle_guid`` (Guid):  [Read-Write] The GUID for this actor's content bundle.
- ``custom_time_dilation`` (float):  [Read-Write] Allow each actor to run at a different time speed. The DeltaTime for a frame is multiplied by the global TimeDilation (in WorldSettings) and this CustomTimeDilation for this actor's tick.
- ``data_layer_assets`` (Array[DataLayerAsset]):  [Read-Write] DataLayers assets the actor belongs to.
- ``data_layers`` (Array[ActorDataLayer]):  [Read-Only] DataLayers the actor belongs to.
- ``default_update_overlaps_method_during_level_streaming`` (ActorUpdateOverlapsMethod):  [Read-Only] Default value taken from config file for this class when 'UseConfigDefault' is chosen for
  'UpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming'. This allows a default to be chosen per class in the matching config.
  For example, for Actor it could be specified in DefaultEngine.ini as:
  
  [/Script/Engine.Actor]
  DefaultUpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming = OnlyUpdateMovable
  
  Another subclass could set their default to something different, such as:
  
  [/Script/Engine.BlockingVolume]
  DefaultUpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming = NeverUpdate
  see: UpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming
- ``enable_auto_lod_generation`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this actor should be considered or not during HLOD generation.
- ``external_data_layer_asset`` (ExternalDataLayerAsset):  [Read-Only]
- ``find_camera_component_when_view_target`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor should search for an owned camera component to view through when used as a view target.
- ``generate_overlap_events_during_level_streaming`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor will generate overlap Begin/End events when spawned as part of level streaming, which includes initial level load.
  You might enable this is in the case where a streaming level loads around an actor and you want Begin/End overlap events to trigger.
  see: UpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming
- ``hidden`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Allows us to only see this Actor in the Editor, and not in the actual game.
  see: SetActorHiddenInGame()
- ``hlod_layer`` (HLODLayer):  [Read-Write] The UHLODLayer in which this actor should be included.
- ``ignores_origin_shifting`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this actor should not be affected by world origin shifting.
- ``initial_life_span`` (float):  [Read-Write] How long this Actor lives before dying, 0=forever. Note this is the INITIAL value and should not be modified once play has begun.
- ``input_priority`` (int32):  [Read-Write] The priority of this input component when pushed in to the stack.
- ``instigator`` (Pawn):  [Read-Write] Pawn responsible for damage and other gameplay events caused by this actor.
- ``is_editor_only_actor`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this actor is editor-only. Use with care, as if this actor is referenced by anything else that reference will be NULL in cooked builds
- ``is_main_world_only`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If checked, this Actor will only get loaded in a main world (persistent level), it will not be loaded through Level Instances.
- ``is_spatially_loaded`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Determine if this actor is spatially loaded when placed in a partitioned world.
       If true, this actor will be loaded when in the range of any streaming sources and if (1) in no data layers, or (2) one or more of its data layers are enabled.
       If false, this actor will be loaded if (1) in no data layers, or (2) one or more of its data layers are enabled.
- ``layers`` (Array[Name]):  [Read-Write] Layers the actor belongs to.  This is outside of the editoronly data to allow hiding of LD-specified layers at runtime for profiling.
- ``migrating_asset`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor can be migrated to another server even if it's been loaded from disk
- ``min_net_update_frequency`` (float):  [Read-Write]
- ``net_cull_distance_squared`` (float):  [Read-Write]
- ``net_dormancy`` (NetDormancy):  [Read-Write] Dormancy setting for actor to take itself off of the replication list without being destroyed on clients.
- ``net_load_on_client`` (bool):  [Read-Write] This actor will be loaded on network clients during map load
- ``net_priority`` (float):  [Read-Write] Priority for this actor when checking for replication in a low bandwidth or saturated situation, higher priority means it is more likely to replicate
- ``net_update_frequency`` (float):  [Read-Write]
- ``net_use_owner_relevancy`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If actor has valid Owner, call Owner's IsNetRelevantFor and GetNetPriority
- ``on_actor_begin_overlap`` (ActorBeginOverlapSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when another actor begins to overlap this actor, for example a player walking into a trigger.
  For events when objects have a blocking collision, for example a player hitting a wall, see 'Hit' events.
  note: Components on both this and the other Actor must have bGenerateOverlapEvents set to true to generate overlap events.
- ``on_actor_end_overlap`` (ActorEndOverlapSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when another actor stops overlapping this actor.
  note: Components on both this and the other Actor must have bGenerateOverlapEvents set to true to generate overlap events.
- ``on_actor_hit`` (ActorHitSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when this Actor hits (or is hit by) something solid. This could happen due to things like Character movement, using Set Location with 'sweep' enabled, or physics simulation.
  For events when objects overlap (e.g. walking into a trigger) see the 'Overlap' event.
  note: For collisions during physics simulation to generate hit events, 'Simulation Generates Hit Events' must be enabled.
- ``on_begin_cursor_over`` (ActorBeginCursorOverSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the mouse cursor is moved over this actor if mouse over events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_clicked`` (ActorOnClickedSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the left mouse button is clicked while the mouse is over this actor and click events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_destroyed`` (ActorDestroyedSignature):  [Read-Write] Event triggered when the actor has been explicitly destroyed.
- ``on_end_cursor_over`` (ActorEndCursorOverSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the mouse cursor is moved off this actor if mouse over events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_end_play`` (ActorEndPlaySignature):  [Read-Write] Event triggered when the actor is being deleted or removed from a level.
- ``on_input_touch_begin`` (ActorOnInputTouchBeginSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when a touch input is received over this actor when touch events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_input_touch_end`` (ActorOnInputTouchEndSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when a touch input is received over this component when touch events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_input_touch_enter`` (ActorBeginTouchOverSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when a finger is moved over this actor when touch over events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_input_touch_leave`` (ActorEndTouchOverSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when a finger is moved off this actor when touch over events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_released`` (ActorOnReleasedSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the left mouse button is released while the mouse is over this actor and click events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_take_any_damage`` (TakeAnyDamageSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the actor is damaged in any way.
- ``on_take_point_damage`` (TakePointDamageSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the actor is damaged by point damage.
- ``on_take_radial_damage`` (TakeRadialDamageSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the actor is damaged by radial damage.
- ``only_relevant_to_owner`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor is only relevant to its owner. If this flag is changed during play, all non-owner channels would need to be explicitly closed.
- ``optimize_bp_component_data`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether to cook additional data to speed up spawn events at runtime for any Blueprint classes based on this Actor. This option may slightly increase memory usage in a cooked build.
- ``physics_replication_mode`` (PhysicsReplicationMode):  [Read-Write] Which mode to replicate physics through for this actor. Only relevant if the actor replicates movement and has a component that simulate physics.
- ``pivot_offset`` (Vector):  [Read-Write] Local space pivot offset for the actor, only used in the editor
- ``primary_actor_tick`` (ActorTickFunction):  [Read-Write] Primary Actor tick function, which calls TickActor().
  Tick functions can be configured to control whether ticking is enabled, at what time during a frame the update occurs, and to set up tick dependencies.
  see: https://docs.unrealengine.com/API/Runtime/Engine/Engine/FTickFunction
  see: AddTickPrerequisiteActor(), AddTickPrerequisiteComponent()
- ``relevant_for_level_bounds`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor's component's bounds will be included in the level's
  bounding box unless the Actor's class has overridden IsLevelBoundsRelevant
- ``remote_role`` (NetRole):  [Read-Only] Describes how much control the remote machine has over the actor.
- ``replay_rewindable`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor will only be destroyed during scrubbing if the replay is set to a time before the actor existed.
  Otherwise, RewindForReplay will be called if we detect the actor needs to be reset.
  Note, this Actor must not be destroyed by gamecode, and RollbackViaDeletion may not be used.
- ``replicate_movement`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, replicate movement/location related properties.
  Actor must also be set to replicate.
  see: SetReplicates()
  see: https://docs.unrealengine.com/InteractiveExperiences/Networking/Actors
- ``replicate_using_registered_sub_object_list`` (bool):  [Read-Write] When true the replication system will only replicate the registered subobjects and the replicated actor components list
  When false the replication system will instead call the virtual ReplicateSubobjects() function where the subobjects and actor components need to be manually replicated.
- ``replicated_movement`` (RepMovement):  [Read-Write] Used for replication of our RootComponent's position and velocity
- ``replicates`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor will replicate to remote machines
  see: SetReplicates()
- ``role`` (NetRole):  [Read-Only] Describes how much control the local machine has over the actor.
- ``root_component`` (SceneComponent):  [Read-Write] The component that defines the transform (location, rotation, scale) of this Actor in the world, all other components must be attached to this one somehow
- ``runtime_grid`` (Name):  [Read-Write] Determine in which partition grid this actor will be placed in the partition (if the world is partitioned).
  If None, the decision will be left to the partition.
- ``spawn_collision_handling_method`` (SpawnActorCollisionHandlingMethod):  [Read-Write] Controls how to handle spawning this actor in a situation where it's colliding with something else. "Default" means AlwaysSpawn here.
- ``sprite_scale`` (float):  [Read-Write] The scale to apply to any billboard components in editor builds (happens in any WITH_EDITOR build, including non-cooked games).
- ``tags`` (Array[Name]):  [Read-Write] Array of tags that can be used for grouping and categorizing.
- ``update_overlaps_method_during_level_streaming`` (ActorUpdateOverlapsMethod):  [Read-Write] Condition for calling UpdateOverlaps() to initialize overlap state when loaded in during level streaming.
  If set to 'UseConfigDefault', the default specified in ini (displayed in 'DefaultUpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming') will be used.
  If overlaps are not initialized, this actor and attached components will not have an initial state of what objects are touching it,
  and overlap events may only come in once one of those objects update overlaps themselves (for example when moving).
  However if an object touching it *does* initialize state, both objects will know about their touching state with each other.
  This can be a potentially large performance savings during level loading and streaming, and is safe if the object and others initially
  overlapping it do not need the overlap state because they will not trigger overlap notifications.
  
  Note that if 'bGenerateOverlapEventsDuringLevelStreaming' is true, overlaps are always updated in this case, but that flag
  determines whether the Begin/End overlap events are triggered.
  see: bGenerateOverlapEventsDuringLevelStreaming, DefaultUpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming, GetUpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming()
```

## unreal.StaticMeshActor

**Best practices:** A thin level actor wrapping a single UStaticMesh; the renderer's cheapest geometry path. You almost never set its mesh on the actor directly — grab its `static_mesh_component` (the root) and call `set_static_mesh(mesh)` / `set_material(idx, mtl)` on that. Spawn with `EditorActorSubsystem.spawn_actor_from_object(static_mesh_asset, location, rotation)` which returns a ready StaticMeshActor. GOTCHA: meshes dragged from the Content Browser auto-become StaticMeshActors, so when querying a level use `EditorActorSubsystem.get_all_level_actors()` + filter by class rather than assuming type. Mobility defaults to Static — to move it at runtime/PIE you must set the component's `mobility` to Movable first. Wrap placement in `ScopedEditorTransaction`; save the level to persist.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/StaticMeshActor_

```text
StaticMeshActor is an instance of a UStaticMesh in the world.
Static meshes are geometry that do not animate or otherwise deform, and are more efficient to render than other types of geometry.
Static meshes dragged into the level from the Content Browser are automatically converted to StaticMeshActors.
see: https://docs.unrealengine.com/latest/INT/Engine/Actors/StaticMeshActor/
see: UStaticMesh

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: Engine
- **File**: StaticMeshActor.h

**Editor Properties:** (see get_editor_property/set_editor_property)

- ``actor_guid`` (Guid):  [Read-Write] The GUID for this actor; this guid will be the same for actors from instanced streaming levels.
  see: ActorInstanceGuid, FActorInstanceGuidMapper
  note: Don't use VisibleAnywhere here to avoid getting the CPF_Edit flag and get this property reset when resetting to defaults. See FActorDetails::AddActorCategory and EditorUtilities::CopySingleProperty for details.
- ``actor_instance_guid`` (Guid):  [Read-Write] The instance GUID for this actor; this guid will be unique for actors from instanced streaming levels.
  see: ActorGuid
  note: This is not guaranteed to be valid during PostLoad, but safe to access from RegisterAllComponents.
- ``allow_tick_before_begin_play`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether we allow this Actor to tick before it receives the BeginPlay event.
  Normally we don't tick actors until after BeginPlay; this setting allows this behavior to be overridden.
  This Actor must be able to tick for this setting to be relevant.
- ``always_relevant`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Always relevant for network (overrides bOnlyRelevantToOwner).
- ``async_physics_tick_enabled`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether to use use the async physics tick with this actor.
- ``auto_destroy_when_finished`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true then destroy self when "finished", meaning all relevant components report that they are done and no timelines or timers are in flight.
- ``auto_receive_input`` (AutoReceiveInput):  [Read-Write] Automatically registers this actor to receive input from a player.
- ``block_input`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, all input on the stack below this actor will not be considered
- ``call_pre_replication`` (bool):  [Read-Write]
- ``call_pre_replication_for_replay`` (bool):  [Read-Write]
- ``can_be_damaged`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this actor can take damage. Must be true for damage events (e.g. ReceiveDamage()) to be called.
  see: https://www.unrealengine.com/blog/damage-in-ue4
  see: TakeDamage(), ReceiveDamage()
- ``can_be_in_cluster`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor can be put inside of a GC Cluster to improve Garbage Collection performance
- ``content_bundle_guid`` (Guid):  [Read-Write] The GUID for this actor's content bundle.
- ``custom_time_dilation`` (float):  [Read-Write] Allow each actor to run at a different time speed. The DeltaTime for a frame is multiplied by the global TimeDilation (in WorldSettings) and this CustomTimeDilation for this actor's tick.
- ``data_layer_assets`` (Array[DataLayerAsset]):  [Read-Write] DataLayers assets the actor belongs to.
- ``data_layers`` (Array[ActorDataLayer]):  [Read-Only] DataLayers the actor belongs to.
- ``default_update_overlaps_method_during_level_streaming`` (ActorUpdateOverlapsMethod):  [Read-Only] Default value taken from config file for this class when 'UseConfigDefault' is chosen for
  'UpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming'. This allows a default to be chosen per class in the matching config.
  For example, for Actor it could be specified in DefaultEngine.ini as:
  
  [/Script/Engine.Actor]
  DefaultUpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming = OnlyUpdateMovable
  
  Another subclass could set their default to something different, such as:
  
  [/Script/Engine.BlockingVolume]
  DefaultUpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming = NeverUpdate
  see: UpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming
- ``enable_auto_lod_generation`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this actor should be considered or not during HLOD generation.
- ``external_data_layer_asset`` (ExternalDataLayerAsset):  [Read-Only]
- ``find_camera_component_when_view_target`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor should search for an owned camera component to view through when used as a view target.
- ``generate_overlap_events_during_level_streaming`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor will generate overlap Begin/End events when spawned as part of level streaming, which includes initial level load.
  You might enable this is in the case where a streaming level loads around an actor and you want Begin/End overlap events to trigger.
  see: UpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming
- ``hidden`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Allows us to only see this Actor in the Editor, and not in the actual game.
  see: SetActorHiddenInGame()
- ``hlod_layer`` (HLODLayer):  [Read-Write] The UHLODLayer in which this actor should be included.
- ``ignores_origin_shifting`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this actor should not be affected by world origin shifting.
- ``initial_life_span`` (float):  [Read-Write] How long this Actor lives before dying, 0=forever. Note this is the INITIAL value and should not be modified once play has begun.
- ``input_priority`` (int32):  [Read-Write] The priority of this input component when pushed in to the stack.
- ``instigator`` (Pawn):  [Read-Write] Pawn responsible for damage and other gameplay events caused by this actor.
- ``is_editor_only_actor`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this actor is editor-only. Use with care, as if this actor is referenced by anything else that reference will be NULL in cooked builds
- ``is_main_world_only`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If checked, this Actor will only get loaded in a main world (persistent level), it will not be loaded through Level Instances.
- ``is_spatially_loaded`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Determine if this actor is spatially loaded when placed in a partitioned world.
       If true, this actor will be loaded when in the range of any streaming sources and if (1) in no data layers, or (2) one or more of its data layers are enabled.
       If false, this actor will be loaded if (1) in no data layers, or (2) one or more of its data layers are enabled.
- ``layers`` (Array[Name]):  [Read-Write] Layers the actor belongs to.  This is outside of the editoronly data to allow hiding of LD-specified layers at runtime for profiling.
- ``migrating_asset`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor can be migrated to another server even if it's been loaded from disk
- ``min_net_update_frequency`` (float):  [Read-Write]
- ``navigation_geometry_gathering_mode`` (NavDataGatheringMode):  [Read-Write]
- ``net_cull_distance_squared`` (float):  [Read-Write]
- ``net_dormancy`` (NetDormancy):  [Read-Write] Dormancy setting for actor to take itself off of the replication list without being destroyed on clients.
- ``net_load_on_client`` (bool):  [Read-Write] This actor will be loaded on network clients during map load
- ``net_priority`` (float):  [Read-Write] Priority for this actor when checking for replication in a low bandwidth or saturated situation, higher priority means it is more likely to replicate
- ``net_update_frequency`` (float):  [Read-Write]
- ``net_use_owner_relevancy`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If actor has valid Owner, call Owner's IsNetRelevantFor and GetNetPriority
- ``on_actor_begin_overlap`` (ActorBeginOverlapSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when another actor begins to overlap this actor, for example a player walking into a trigger.
  For events when objects have a blocking collision, for example a player hitting a wall, see 'Hit' events.
  note: Components on both this and the other Actor must have bGenerateOverlapEvents set to true to generate overlap events.
- ``on_actor_end_overlap`` (ActorEndOverlapSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when another actor stops overlapping this actor.
  note: Components on both this and the other Actor must have bGenerateOverlapEvents set to true to generate overlap events.
- ``on_actor_hit`` (ActorHitSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when this Actor hits (or is hit by) something solid. This could happen due to things like Character movement, using Set Location with 'sweep' enabled, or physics simulation.
  For events when objects overlap (e.g. walking into a trigger) see the 'Overlap' event.
  note: For collisions during physics simulation to generate hit events, 'Simulation Generates Hit Events' must be enabled.
- ``on_begin_cursor_over`` (ActorBeginCursorOverSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the mouse cursor is moved over this actor if mouse over events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_clicked`` (ActorOnClickedSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the left mouse button is clicked while the mouse is over this actor and click events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_destroyed`` (ActorDestroyedSignature):  [Read-Write] Event triggered when the actor has been explicitly destroyed.
- ``on_end_cursor_over`` (ActorEndCursorOverSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the mouse cursor is moved off this actor if mouse over events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_end_play`` (ActorEndPlaySignature):  [Read-Write] Event triggered when the actor is being deleted or removed from a level.
- ``on_input_touch_begin`` (ActorOnInputTouchBeginSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when a touch input is received over this actor when touch events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_input_touch_end`` (ActorOnInputTouchEndSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when a touch input is received over this component when touch events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_input_touch_enter`` (ActorBeginTouchOverSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when a finger is moved over this actor when touch over events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_input_touch_leave`` (ActorEndTouchOverSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when a finger is moved off this actor when touch over events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_released`` (ActorOnReleasedSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the left mouse button is released while the mouse is over this actor and click events are enabled in the player controller.
- ``on_take_any_damage`` (TakeAnyDamageSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the actor is damaged in any way.
- ``on_take_point_damage`` (TakePointDamageSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the actor is damaged by point damage.
- ``on_take_radial_damage`` (TakeRadialDamageSignature):  [Read-Write] Called when the actor is damaged by radial damage.
- ``only_relevant_to_owner`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor is only relevant to its owner. If this flag is changed during play, all non-owner channels would need to be explicitly closed.
- ``optimize_bp_component_data`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether to cook additional data to speed up spawn events at runtime for any Blueprint classes based on this Actor. This option may slightly increase memory usage in a cooked build.
- ``physics_replication_mode`` (PhysicsReplicationMode):  [Read-Write] Which mode to replicate physics through for this actor. Only relevant if the actor replicates movement and has a component that simulate physics.
- ``pivot_offset`` (Vector):  [Read-Write] Local space pivot offset for the actor, only used in the editor
- ``primary_actor_tick`` (ActorTickFunction):  [Read-Write] Primary Actor tick function, which calls TickActor().
  Tick functions can be configured to control whether ticking is enabled, at what time during a frame the update occurs, and to set up tick dependencies.
  see: https://docs.unrealengine.com/API/Runtime/Engine/Engine/FTickFunction
  see: AddTickPrerequisiteActor(), AddTickPrerequisiteComponent()
- ``relevant_for_level_bounds`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor's component's bounds will be included in the level's
  bounding box unless the Actor's class has overridden IsLevelBoundsRelevant
- ``remote_role`` (NetRole):  [Read-Only] Describes how much control the remote machine has over the actor.
- ``replay_rewindable`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor will only be destroyed during scrubbing if the replay is set to a time before the actor existed.
  Otherwise, RewindForReplay will be called if we detect the actor needs to be reset.
  Note, this Actor must not be destroyed by gamecode, and RollbackViaDeletion may not be used.
- ``replicate_movement`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, replicate movement/location related properties.
  Actor must also be set to replicate.
  see: SetReplicates()
  see: https://docs.unrealengine.com/InteractiveExperiences/Networking/Actors
- ``replicate_using_registered_sub_object_list`` (bool):  [Read-Write] When true the replication system will only replicate the registered subobjects and the replicated actor components list
  When false the replication system will instead call the virtual ReplicateSubobjects() function where the subobjects and actor components need to be manually replicated.
- ``replicated_movement`` (RepMovement):  [Read-Write] Used for replication of our RootComponent's position and velocity
- ``replicates`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, this actor will replicate to remote machines
  see: SetReplicates()
- ``role`` (NetRole):  [Read-Only] Describes how much control the local machine has over the actor.
- ``root_component`` (SceneComponent):  [Read-Write] The component that defines the transform (location, rotation, scale) of this Actor in the world, all other components must be attached to this one somehow
- ``runtime_grid`` (Name):  [Read-Write] Determine in which partition grid this actor will be placed in the partition (if the world is partitioned).
  If None, the decision will be left to the partition.
- ``spawn_collision_handling_method`` (SpawnActorCollisionHandlingMethod):  [Read-Write] Controls how to handle spawning this actor in a situation where it's colliding with something else. "Default" means AlwaysSpawn here.
- ``sprite_scale`` (float):  [Read-Write] The scale to apply to any billboard components in editor builds (happens in any WITH_EDITOR build, including non-cooked games).
- ``static_mesh_component`` (StaticMeshComponent):  [Read-Only]
- ``static_mesh_physics_replication_mode`` (PhysicsReplicationMode):  [Read-Write] Set which replication mode to use for this static mesh instance if it both replicates and simulates physics.
- ``static_mesh_replicate_movement`` (bool):  [Read-Write] This static mesh should replicate movement. Automatically sets the RemoteRole and bReplicateMovement flags. Meant to be edited on placed actors (those other two properties are not)
- ``tags`` (Array[Name]):  [Read-Write] Array of tags that can be used for grouping and categorizing.
- ``update_overlaps_method_during_level_streaming`` (ActorUpdateOverlapsMethod):  [Read-Write] Condition for calling UpdateOverlaps() to initialize overlap state when loaded in during level streaming.
  If set to 'UseConfigDefault', the default specified in ini (displayed in 'DefaultUpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming') will be used.
  If overlaps are not initialized, this actor and attached components will not have an initial state of what objects are touching it,
  and overlap events may only come in once one of those objects update overlaps themselves (for example when moving).
  However if an object touching it *does* initialize state, both objects will know about their touching state with each other.
  This can be a potentially large performance savings during level loading and streaming, and is safe if the object and others initially
  overlapping it do not need the overlap state because they will not trigger overlap notifications.
  
  Note that if 'bGenerateOverlapEventsDuringLevelStreaming' is true, overlaps are always updated in this case, but that flag
  determines whether the Begin/End overlap events are triggered.
  see: bGenerateOverlapEventsDuringLevelStreaming, DefaultUpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming, GetUpdateOverlapsMethodDuringLevelStreaming()
```

## unreal.StaticMeshComponent

**Best practices:** The render+collision component that holds a StaticMesh asset reference plus per-instance material overrides. Key methods: `set_static_mesh(mesh)` (verified in UE5.7 headers, returns bool), and inherited `set_material(index, material_interface)` / `get_material(index)` / `get_num_materials()` for per-instance overrides that do NOT touch the source asset. GOTCHA: overrides set here live on the component, not the StaticMesh — to change the asset's default materials edit the StaticMesh's `static_materials` instead. Changing the mesh on a component whose `mobility` is Static while the actor is in a built world can require a registration refresh; for editor scripts it's fine. All transform/visibility edits go through its SceneComponent base (set_world_location etc.) on the game thread inside a transaction.  
_Docs: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/StaticMeshComponent_

```text
StaticMeshComponent is used to create an instance of a UStaticMesh.
A static mesh is a piece of geometry that consists of a static set of polygons.
see: https://docs.unrealengine.com/latest/INT/Engine/Content/Types/StaticMeshes/
see: UStaticMesh

**C++ Source:**

- **Module**: Engine
- **File**: StaticMeshComponent.h

**Editor Properties:** (see get_editor_property/set_editor_property)

- ``absolute_location`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If RelativeLocation should be considered relative to the world, rather than the parent
- ``absolute_rotation`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If RelativeRotation should be considered relative to the world, rather than the parent
- ``absolute_scale`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If RelativeScale3D should be considered relative to the world, rather than the parent
- ``affect_distance_field_lighting`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Controls whether the primitive should affect dynamic distance field lighting methods.  This flag is only used if CastShadow is true.
- ``affect_dynamic_indirect_lighting`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Controls whether the primitive should influence indirect lighting.
- ``affect_indirect_lighting_while_hidden`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Controls whether the primitive should affect indirect lighting when hidden. This flag is only used if bAffectDynamicIndirectLighting is true.
- ``allow_cull_distance_volume`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether to accept cull distance volumes to modify cached cull distance.
- ``always_create_physics_state`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Indicates if we'd like to create physics state all the time (for collision and simulation).
  If you set this to false, it still will create physics state if collision or simulation activated.
  This can help performance if you'd like to avoid overhead of creating physics state when triggers
- ``apply_impulse_on_damage`` (bool):  [Read-Write] True for damage to this component to apply physics impulse, false to opt out of these impulses.
- ``asset_user_data`` (Array[AssetUserData]):  [Read-Write] Array of user data stored with the component
- ``asset_user_data_editor_only`` (Array[AssetUserData]):  [Read-Write] Array of user data stored with the component
- ``auto_activate`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether the component is activated at creation or must be explicitly activated.
- ``body_instance`` (BodyInstance):  [Read-Write] Physics scene information for this component, holds a single rigid body with multiple shapes.
- ``bounds_scale`` (float):  [Read-Write] Scales the bounds of the object.
  This is useful when using World Position Offset to animate the vertices of the object outside of its bounds.
  Warning: Increasing the bounds of an object will reduce performance and shadow quality!
  Currently only used by StaticMeshComponent and SkeletalMeshComponent.
- ``cached_max_draw_distance`` (float):  [Read-Only] The distance to cull this primitive at.
  A CachedMaxDrawDistance of 0 indicates that the primitive should not be culled by distance.
- ``can_character_step_up_on`` (CanBeCharacterBase):  [Read-Write] Determine whether a Character can step up onto this component.
  This controls whether they can try to step up on it when they bump in to it, not whether they can walk on it after landing on it.
  see: FWalkableSlopeOverride
- ``can_ever_affect_navigation`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this component can potentially influence navigation
- ``cast_cinematic_shadow`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this component should cast shadows from lights that have bCastShadowsFromCinematicObjectsOnly enabled.
  This is useful for characters in a cinematic with special cinematic lights, where the cost of shadowmap rendering of the environment is undesired.
- ``cast_contact_shadow`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether the object should cast contact shadows.
  This flag is only used if CastShadow is true.
- ``cast_distance_field_indirect_shadow`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether to use the mesh distance field representation (when present) for shadowing indirect lighting (from lightmaps or skylight) on Movable components.
  This works like capsule shadows on skeletal meshes, except using the mesh distance field so no physics asset is required.
  The StaticMesh must have 'Generate Mesh Distance Field' enabled, or the project must have 'Generate Mesh Distance Fields' enabled for this feature to work.
- ``cast_dynamic_shadow`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Controls whether the primitive should cast shadows in the case of non precomputed shadowing. This flag is only used if CastShadow is true and if FirstPersonPrimitiveType is not set to FirstPerson.
- ``cast_far_shadow`` (bool):  [Read-Write] When enabled, the component will be rendering into the far shadow cascades (only for directional lights). This flag is only used if CastShadow is true and if FirstPersonPrimitiveType is not set to FirstPerson.
- ``cast_hidden_shadow`` (bool):  [Read-Write] If true, the primitive will cast shadows even if bHidden is true.
  Controls whether the primitive should cast shadows when hidden.
  This flag is only used if CastShadow is true and if FirstPersonPrimitiveType is not set to WorldSpaceRepresentation.
- ``cast_inset_shadow`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this component should create a per-object shadow that gives higher effective shadow resolution.
  Useful for cinematic character shadowing. Assumed to be enabled if bSelfShadowOnly is enabled.
- ``cast_shadow`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Controls whether the primitive component should cast a shadow or not.
- ``cast_shadow_as_two_sided`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether this primitive should cast dynamic shadows as if it were a two sided material.
- ``cast_static_shadow`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether the object should cast a static shadow from shadow casting lights. This flag is only used if CastShadow is true and if FirstPersonPrimitiveType is not set to FirstPerson.
- ``cast_volumetric_translucent_shadow`` (bool):  [Read-Write] Whether the object should cast a volumetric translucent shadow.
  Volumetric translucent shadows are useful for primitives with smoothly changing opacity like particles representing a volume,
  but have artifacts when used on highly opaque surfaces. This flag is only used if CastShadow is true and if FirstPersonPrimitiveType is not set to FirstPerson.
- ``component_tags`` (Array[Name]):  [Read-Write] Array of tags that can be used for grouping and categorizing. Can also be accessed from scripting.
```
